## Document 031: 2026-10-29_［みずほ経済インサイト］岐路に立つ米国AI投資ブーム_2f600e53

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-29_［みずほ経済インサイト］岐路に立つ米国AI投資ブーム_2f600e53.pdf
Report source: Mizuho

Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

© 2026 Mizuho Bank, Ltd.みずほ総合研究所はみずほ銀行内の組織の名称です。
岐路に立つ米国AI投資ブーム
～持続に向けた必要条件と目先のリスク点検～
2026年10月29日
みずほ総合研究所
調査部
【みずほ経済インサイト】

### Page 2

 AI市場の成長期待を背景に、競争優位の確保に向けた巨額投資と、それに伴うAI技術の高
度化が進展
 投資資金の確保に向けて、社債発行など外部資金調達が活発化。こうした資金需要の拡大
が金利上昇の一因となり、企業の資金調達コストは増大
 AI関連の投資競争が続くなか、AIブームは企業の選別・再編が進む新たな局面へ。その過程
で経営資源が競争力の高い企業に引き継がれ、AI市場全体の成長は継続すると予想
 性能向上と利用コストの低下により、AIの活用可能な領域は拡大する見込み。ユーザー企業
間の競争がAIの普及を加速させ、市場の持続的な成長を後押し
 AI市場の中核を担う企業の財務は健全。金融システム全体が不安定化するリスクは限定的
 一方、AIインフラ運営企業は将来的な財務負担が高まりやすい構造。特にネオクラウドでは、
減価償却費の負担が利益を圧迫するほか、2030年頃には設備更新や債務借換えが資金繰
りの重荷に。市場による企業評価は一段と厳格化し、投融資先の選別が進む可能性
要旨

（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
巨額のAI投資は金融環境にどのような変化をもたらしているか？（P4～6）ポイント①
AI開発・インフラ企業の脆弱性はどこに・どの程度あるのか？（P7～10）ポイント②
AIブームは持続するのか？（P11～12）ポイント③

AIを巡る競争は白熱。AIの性能向上と巨額投資が続く
 AI市場の成長期待が高まるなか、先端AI企業間の技術開発・投資競争に拍車がかかる
― AIモデルの高度化が進み、性能指標は過去数年で飛躍的に上昇
― 競争優位の確保に向けた巨額の設備投資が継続。AI関連企業の投資は2026年時点で1.2兆ドルに到達し、アナリスト
は、今後も高水準で推移すると予想

企業の資金余力は潤沢も、巨額の投資に備え更なる資金確保の動き
 AI投資は過熱感を帯びつつあるものの、資金枯渇によるクラッシュの可能性は低い
― 米国非金融法人は投資増を上回る内部資金の増加によりマクロで資金余剰を維持。投資継続を支えるバッファーは十分
― ITバブル期は企業部門が資金不足に傾くなかで投資を拡大したが、今回は内部資金が潤沢
 もっとも、米国非金融法人の外部資金調達（社債発行・銀行借入等）は活発化
― 調達増は内部資金不足の補填というより、巨額の先行投資に備えた更なる資金確保を反映している可能性

AI市場を担う主要企業の財務基盤は健全。AIインフラ運営企業は財務負担が高まりやすい構造
 AI市場をけん引するハイパースケーラー、半導体開発・製造企業の財務基盤は比較的健全
― これらの企業はAI関連企業の売上高の78.8％、債務の55.0％を占めているため、直ちに金融システム全体の不安定化
につながるとは考えにくい
 一方、データセンター運営やネオクラウド（GPUサーバー運営）などのAIインフラ運営企業は財務負担が高まりやすい構造

― これらの企業では、投資回収の遅れや資金調達環境の悪化により財務リスクが顕在化するおそれ
（注） 1. ハイパースケーラーは、大規模なクラウド基盤を運営する巨大IT企業。ネオクラウドは、GPU（AIの学習・推論などに用いる半導体）サーバーを運用し、計算資源を提供する企業
2. 電力供給企業など表に記載していない分類があるため、構成比の合計は100％とならない
（出所） LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

ネオクラウドの減価償却負担の増加が、利益改善と株式評価の重荷に
 AIインフラ運営企業のうち、特にネオクラウドでは、先行投資に伴う減価償却費の大幅な増加が見込まれる。売上拡大に
よって償却負担を吸収できるかが、今後の利益改善を左右
 利益改善が市場予想を下回れば、株式評価の悪化も
― GPUの急速な技術進歩による早期陳腐化も懸念材料。利益の黒字化が市場予想より遅れた場合には、株価下落や増

資条件の悪化につながる可能性

満期到来で
資金需要増
巨額投資に伴うFCF赤字が続くなか、設備更新と債務償還の重なりが資金繰りを圧迫する懸念
 巨額の先行投資を背景に、ネオクラウドのフリーキャッシュフロー（FCF、営業キャッシュフローから設備投資額を差し引いて
計算）は、今後数年間の赤字が見込まれる。手元資金が減少し、追加資金調達の必要性が高まる可能性
 設備更新と債務償還の重なりが資金繰りの負担に
― GPUの耐用年数は概ね5～6年。また、ネオクラウドが発行する社債の年限は5年程度。十分な資金を確保できなければ、

投資の縮小や事業拡大の足踏みにつながるリスク

完値を含む
2. 各年は年度ベースで表記しており、決算期の違いにより対象期間にずれがある

### Page 10

2030
年頃に更新投資が必要に
AIインフラ運営企業の収益性に対する評価は、数年以内により厳しくなる見込み
 AIインフラ運営企業は今後数年以内に収益化を果たせるかが焦点。市場では2030年頃のリスクを見据え選別が進む見込み
― 経営への懸念が強まるネオクラウドは、フリーキャッシュフローの赤字が続く見込み。減価償却負担の増加が利益改善の
重荷となるなか、2030年頃には債務の借換えや半導体の更新投資が重なり、資金繰りが厳しくなる懸念
― 半導体の性能向上や価格低下により、既存設備の競争力が低下し、投資回収が遅れるリスクも
ネオクラウドのリスクを測る注目指標

AI投資ブームはまだ入口。「終わりの始まり」ではなく、「始まりの終わり」
 AI投資ブームは、競争激化に伴い企業の選別・再編が進む新たな局面へ
― マクロでみた設備過剰感は初期段階。今後もモデル開発や計算基盤を巡る競争が激化するなか、資金の出し手や事業
パートナーによる選別は一段と厳格化。一部企業では経営破綻や事業再編が進む可能性
― もっとも、個別企業の淘汰はAI技術の衰退を意味せず。設備・顧客・人材などの経営資源が競争力の高い企業へ再配
分されることで、AIの発展・普及は続く見込み

（注） 1994～2000年は2002年当時の公表値。2026年は見込み値
（出所） 米国商務省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
米国：IT機器に関するストック循環図 AI関連企業の選別と再編（イメージ）
マクロの視点では設備過剰感が出るのはこれから
11
（出所）みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

設備過剰感が強い

急激な投資選別が進行
設備・顧客・人材が再配分され、
AIエコシステムは発展継続

31 過去2週間でAIを利用した

（回答割合、％）
（年/月）
AIの普及は不可逆な潮流。技術進歩に加え、ユーザー企業間の競争が導入を促進
 技術進歩によるAI導入の経済的メリット拡大と、ユーザー企業間の競争圧力がAI実装を後押し
― 性能向上や利用コストの低下によって導入のハードルが下がり、AI活用のすそ野は拡大
― AIを活用する企業が増えるなか、業界内の競争に取り残されないためにAI導入の必要性が高まる
 AIの利用実績・利用意向はともに上昇傾向にあり、今後も企業への普及が進む見込み
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

## Document 071: 2026年10月宏观数据点评：10月宏观数据有所下行，新旧动能转换持续推进

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月宏观数据点评：10月宏观数据有所下行，新旧动能转换持续推进.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
10月宏观数据有所下行，新旧动能转换持续推进 
——2026年 10月宏观数据点评 
首席宏观分析师 王青  研究发展部执行总监 冯琳 
事件：据国家统计局2026年11月16日公布的数据，10月规模以上工业增加值同比实际增速为4.8%，
9月为5.5%，1-10月为5.3%，去年全年为5.9%；10月社会消费品零售总额同比增速为0.4%，9月为1.1%，
1-10月为 1.0%，去年全年为 3.7%；1-10月全国固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，前值为-7.2%，去年全
年为-3.8%。 
基本观点： 
总体上看，主要受中秋错期导致居民消费增速回落，前期出台的稳投资政策需要传导时间，以及外
部扰动加剧等影响，10月各类宏观数据普遍有所下行。不过，当月高技术制造业生产增速加快，相关投
资保持正增长，居民消费中的旅游出行、文体休闲消费延续较快增长势头，显示新动能对宏观经济增长
的拉动力进一步增强。工业生产方面，10月工业增加值增速较上月放缓，“中秋错期”是直接扰动，同
时，内需偏弱与外部扰动加剧导致能源成本上行，进一步加大工业生产下行压力，但当月出口高增与新
动能行业生产较快扩张，仍对工业生产形成有力支撑。10月社零同比增速回落，主要受“中秋错期”因
素影响。同时，在补贴政策拉动效果减弱、房地产市场持续下行、居民消费信心不足等因素叠加影响下，
当月消费增长动能边际转弱，也是社零增速放缓的重要原因。1-10月固定资产投资同比增速为-8.1%，降
幅较前值扩大 0.9个百分点。9月以来稳投资政策全面发力，但从资金投放到形成实物工作量需要传导时
间，这是 10月基建投资、制造业投资未能遏制降幅扩大势头的主要原因。受近期新房市场延续深度调整
等影响，10月房地产投资下行加快，符合预期。不过，前10个月知识产权产品投资同比增长9.2%，高技
术产业投资保持较快正增长，显示投资结构优化。 
往后看，年底前出口仍然有望保持 20%以上的高增速，9月末以来系列稳增长政策出台后，接下来投
资降幅有收敛趋势，低基数下居民消费增速也将有所回升。这将带动工业生产增速上行，服务业生产将
保持较快增长，当前比较稳定的就业形势将会延续。初步估计，四季度 GDP增速将加快至 4.6%左右（三
季度为 4.5%），全年 GDP增速将在 4.6%附近，落在“4.5%至5.0%”的目标范围之内。由此我们预计，尽
管 10月宏观数据有所回落，但年底前出台新一轮稳增长政策的迫切性不高；着眼于稳定明年一季度宏观
经济运行，后期包括全面降息在内，各类总量型稳增长政策都有发力空间。

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
   具体解读如下： 
一、10月工业生产增速较上月明显放缓，“中秋错期”是直接扰动，同时，内需偏弱与能源成本上
行进一步加大生产下行压力；但出口高增与新动能行业生产较快扩张，仍对工业生产形成有力支撑。  
10月规上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月回落 0.7个百分点。分门类看，采矿业、制造业、
电力燃气及水的生产和供应业增加值同比分别增长0.7%、5.4%和4.4%，增速较上月分别放缓0.6、0.6和
1.2个百分点。 
10月工业生产增速放缓，主要受三方面因素影响：一是“中秋错期”导致今年 9月工作日比去年同
期多一天，而 10月工作日天数则与去年 10月相同，工业生产同比增速相应回落。二是 10月国内投资降
幅扩大、消费增速放缓，内需对工业生产构成一定抑制。三是 10月外部扰动加剧，国际原油价格上涨，
国内煤炭价格也有一定幅度上行，成本上升对部分行业生产形成压力。这主要体现在当月化学原料及化
学制品制造业增加值同比增速从上月的 2.4%放缓至 1.3%，以及从今年 4月起，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加
工业增加值已连续6个月同比负增。 
不过，与投资和消费走势相比，10 月工业生产、尤其是制造业生产仍维持较快增长。这主要受出口
高增以及新动能领域生产持续较快增长拉动。一方面，10月规上企业出口交货值同比增长18.6%，增速较
上月加快 1.6个百分点，对生产的拉动进一步增强。当月出口高增主要受集成电路出口带动，在此背景
下，10月计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值同比增速较上月加快3.0个百分点至19.4%；另一方
面，在市场供需两旺、财政金融政策支持力度持续加大的有利环境下，10 月高技术产业增加值同比增长
17.6%，增速较上月加快 2.3个百分点，装备制造业增加值同比增长 10.6%，均显著高于规上工业增加值
总体增速，对整体工业生产形成较为强劲的拉动作用。 
展望 11月，出口将延续高增，国内稳增长政策发力有望带动投资改善，高技术制造业生产将延续较
快增长，叠加今年 11月工作日比去年同期多一天，工业生产增速总体上将有所回升，预计当月工业增加
值同比增速将小幅加快至5.0%左右。 
三、10月社零同比增速回落，主要受“中秋错期”因素影响。同时，在补贴政策拉动效果减弱、房
地产市场持续下行、居民消费信心不足等因素叠加影响下，当月消费增长动能边际转弱，也是社零增速
放缓的重要原因。 
10月社零同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月回落 0.7个百分点。分项看，商品零售和餐饮收入同比分别增
长0.3%和0.7%，增速较上月分别放缓0.7和1.4个百分点。当月社零增速明显回落，主要受“中秋错期”

### Page 3

东方金诚宏观研究

3 
影响。去年中秋假期落在 10月上旬并与国庆连休，今年则位于 9月中旬，假日消费在两个月份之间重新
分布，对 9月同比形成正向支撑，对 10月同比则产生一定下拉作用。同时，从环比来看，10月社零季调
环比增长 0.02%，增速低于 9月的 0.35%，也低于过去十年同期 0.45%的平均水平，显示消费增长动能依
然偏弱，这主要受补贴政策拉动效果减弱、房地产市场持续下行、居民消费信心不足等因素叠加影响。 
从限额以上商品零售额看，主要受中秋错期影响，10 月限额以上各类别商品零售额增速普遍放缓。
必需消费品中，粮油食品、中西药品零售额同比增速分别降至0.5%（前值6.7%）和3.7%（前值5.0%），
日用品零售额同比下降 0.4%（前值增长 4.1%）。可选消费品中，化妆品、通讯器材零售额同比增速分别
回落至2.8%（前值5.4%）和6.5%（前值31.1%）；服装鞋帽零售额同比下降2.0%（前值增长1.2%）；金
银珠宝、汽车零售额同比降幅分别扩大至 27.7%和 21.4%。不过，受去年同期基数走低影响，10月家电零
售额同比增速加快至 2.5%（前值 1.9%），体育娱乐用品零售额同比增长 1.0%（前值-1.1%），家具零售
额同比降幅收窄至 2.6%（前值-3.0%）；另外，10月石油制品零售额同比增长 4.4%（前值-0.8%），主要
与当月成品油价格调升有关。 
二季度以来，社零表现总体走弱，服务消费增速亦有所走低——1-10 月服务零售额累计同比增长
4.9%，增速较前值放缓0.1个百分点，主要与补贴政策退坡、房地产市场延续下行等因素有关。其中，房
地产市场下行是主要拖累，这不仅直接抑制涉房消费，也在持续影响居民消费信心。数据显示，9月消费
者信心指数录得89.0，较8月下滑0.5个点，仍明显低于100的中值水平。同时值得关注的是，今年1-9
月城镇居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 4.5%，增速较一季度加快 0.3个百分点，但人均可支配收入中
位数同比增速较一季度放缓 0.5个百分点至 3.9%——收入均值与中位数增速背离，表明中低收入群体收
入改善相对滞后，这也是二季度以来消费增速总体放缓的重要原因。 
展望后续，随着“中秋错期”因素消退、上年同期基数走低，预计 11月社零同比增速将有所回升；
同时，前 10个月，旅游咨询租赁服务类、文体休闲服务类零售额明显快于服务零售额增长，对服务消费
带动作用比较明显，预计接下来政策层面还将持续推动文旅、赛事、康养等领域的消费潜力释放。但在
居民消费信心不足背景下，消费偏弱局面短期内仍难以扭转，预计 11月社零同比增速将在 1.6%附近。 
三、投资：1-10月固定资产投资同比增速为-8.1%，降幅较前值扩大 0.9个百分点。9月以来稳投资
政策全面发力，但从资金投放到形成实物工作量需要传导时间，这是 10月基建投资、制造业投资未能遏
制降幅扩大势头的主要原因。近期新房市场延续深度调整，10月房地产投资下行加快，符合预期。不过，
前 10个月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，高技术产业投资保持较快正增长，显示投资结构优化。

### Page 4

东方金诚宏观研究

4 
1-10月基建投资同比下降 5.1%，降幅比前值扩大 0.9个百分点，不及市场预期。10月 8000亿元新
型政策性金融工具投放完毕；当月安排使用地方政府债务结存限额 5500亿元，其中专项债务结存限额
2500亿元，并向经济大省倾斜，支持有关地区优先保障在建项目资金需求；近期“六张网”建设部署加
快，央行在9月末宣布降低PSL利率，将“六张网”建设纳入支持范围，同时，近期政府债券发行明显提
速，且用于项目建设的资金比例上升。以上稳投资措施均会对基建投资有直接促进作用。不过，上述政
策推进过程中，从资金投放到形成实物工作量需要传导时间，加之受土地出让金大幅下行影响，当前地
方财力普遍偏紧，或是 1-10月基建投资降幅进一步扩大的主要原因。 
往后看，伴随前述稳投资政策效果逐步显现，1-11月基建投资降幅有望收敛至-4.7%左右——数据显
示，10月水泥产量同比下降1.3%，降幅连续两个月收窄，且明显小于前10个月-7.9%的累计降幅。总体
上看，考虑到今年稳投资政策发力时间较去年有所提前，四季度基建投资降幅仍有望显著收窄。这是年
底前“加大逆周期调节力度”的主要体现。 
制造业投资方面，1-10月累计同比增速为-2.9%，降幅较前值扩大 0.8个百分点。背后可能有三个原
因：一是今年 8000亿元新型政策性金融工具支持范围包括先进制造业投资，但从资金投放到形成实物工
作量需要时间。这是 10月制造业投资不及市场预期的主要原因。二是年初以来工业企业利润回暖，主要
得益于全球 AI投资热潮提振电子、有色行业盈利，叠加国际油价上行推高石油石化产业链利润，其他多
数工业行业利润同比下行幅度在加剧。其中，1-9月汽车制造业利润同比下降 22.8%，增速较去年大幅下
行23.4个百分点；这导致1-10月汽车制造业投资同比下降7.9%，而去年全年同比增长11.7%。三是最新
数据显示，2026年三季度工业产能利用率降至 73.1%，环比上升 0.1个百分点，但仍处在近 10年中除
2020年一季度特殊时点外的低位附近，主要受煤炭、水泥、汽车、电气机械和器材制造业等行业产能利
用率下行影响。工业产能利用率偏低会对制造业投资形成较强约束。 
不过，受市场供需两旺及政策倾斜力度较大带动，今年以来高技术制造业生产保持两位数高增，1-10
月高技术产业投资同比增长4.0%，其中，铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业投资增长14.1%，
计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业投资增长 9.9%。另外，1-10月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，在整
体固定资产投资中的占比已升至 15%左右。这些领域投资高增，不仅有利于当前增加高质量供给，而且能
够夯实长期发展基础，显示当前制造业投资结构在持续改善。往看后，接下来 8000亿新型政策性金融工
具、今年超长期特别国债等支持的制造业项目投资会加快推进，对制造业投资的拉动作用会逐步显现。
我们判断，1-11月制造业投资下行势头会转向收窄。 
房地产投资方面，1-10月累计同比增速为-21.6%，降幅较前值扩大 1.0个百分点。近期房地产投资

### Page 5

东方金诚宏观研究

5 
降幅持续扩大，背后是二季度以来新房市场延续调整。另外，当前金融机构对房企融资仍然偏于谨慎。 
9月 29日，全国范围内针对刚需购房实施定向贴息的政策出台，对楼市有一定提振作用，但主要集
中在二手房市场，10月新房市场仍然偏弱。同时，8月 28日公布系列房地产新政，主要内容包括推动商
品房现售，完善房地产开发贷管理，延长居民按揭贷期限等，核心是将“房住不炒”制度化，加快构建
房地产行业发展新模式。这在引导房地产行业长期稳定发展的同时，会在短期内加大房企资金压力，也
可能是近期房地产投资降幅扩大的一个原因。我们预计，1-11月房地产投资同比降幅有可能扩大至22.0%
左右。总体上看，年底前房地产投资降幅收窄的难度很大。

---
## Document 075: 2026年10月经济数据点评：新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压

### Page 1

1 
[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 16 日      
证 券研究报告•宏观简评报告     
数据点评 
新兴产业与外需支撑景气，内需修复持续承压 
——2026 年 10 月经济数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院 
[Table_Summary]  摘要：工业生产方面，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月
回落 0.7 个百分点，低于市场预期。高技术制造业和装备制造业增加值分别增
长 17.6%、10.6%，其中高技术制造业创近五年来单月增速新高，AI相关电子
硬件、工业机器人、3D 打印设备等产品产量保持较快增长。同时，10 月规上
工业出口交货值增长 18.6%，外需仍是工业生产的重要支撑。整体工业生产呈
现总量放缓、结构分化的特征，高技术制造和出口链条景气度较高，传统周期
性行业修复相对滞后。固定资产投资方面，1-10 月全国固定资产投资同比下降
8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点，投资需求仍处于深度收缩区间。基建
投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资下降 5.1%，专项债发行节奏放缓、资金向实
物工作量转化存在时滞，整体托底效应尚未充分显现，但互联网和相关服务业
投资，以及交通运输、电力供应等重点领域投资仍保持正增长。随着专项债剩
余额度加快使用及“两重”项目建设推进，重点基建领域投资有望获得一定支
撑，但扭转整体投资下行趋势仍面临较大难度。房地产投资方面，1-10 月房地
产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点，地产仍是整体
投资的重要拖累。供给端施工、竣工面积单月同比分别下降 32.5%、39.1%，
降幅均有所扩大，新开工降幅有所收窄但仍处负增长区间；需求端商品房销售
面积、销售额分别下降 20.7%、20.5%，降幅小幅收窄，但销售修复尚未有效
传导至房企投资和施工活动。房企资金来源继续收缩，行业投资意愿和项目建
设规模仍然偏弱，预计年内地产投资仍处深度调整阶段，降幅收窄时点或推迟
至 2027 年。制造业投资方面，1-10 月制造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9
月扩大 0.8 个百分点，企业资本开支意愿仍然偏弱。高技术制造业投资增长
3.0%，电子电路、锂离子电池和集成电路制造业投资增速较快，科技产业投资
仍具韧性。“两新”政策继续支撑设备更新，设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，
占全部投资比重提升至 20.3%。但汽车、通用设备等装备制造行业投资明显走
弱，反映生产端韧性尚未转化为新增资本开支。消费方面，10 月社会消费品
零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，消费需求整体偏弱。
汽车类零售同比下降 21.4%，仍是社零增长的主要拖累；通讯器材、金银珠宝
等品类受前期高基数及金价波动影响，零售增速明显回落，家电、家具消费则
受低基数影响边际改善。服务消费相对更具韧性，1-10 月服务零售额增长
4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，汽车及地产相关消费能否改善、
以旧换新政策效应能否延续，仍是影响商品消费修复的重要因素，服务消费则
有望继续成为居民消费的重要支撑。 
 工业生产总量边际降温，科技制造与出口链条维持高景气。2026 年 10 月，全
国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点，低于
wind 一致预期的 5.32%。分行业看，采矿业、制造业、电力、热力、燃气及
水生产和供应业的工业增加值同比较上月均边际走弱，增速分别回落至 0.7%、
5.4%、4.4%。但制造业工业增加值同比相比近十年季节性水平略高，表明生
产端并未出现明显失速。结构上，10 月高技术产业、装备制造业工业增加值
同比分别增长 17.6%、10.6%。高技术产业增速创下近 5 年来单月最高增速，
其中电子工业专用设备制造、计算机外围设备制造、电子器件制造等行业增加
值分别增长 46.8%、44.7%、34.8%，显示电子信息产业链仍处于较高景气区
间。装备制造业 1-10 月累计增加值占全部规模以上工业比重为 37.9%，同比
提高 1.8 个百分点。细分行业中，10 月单月，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制

2
造业、专用设备制造业、石油和天然气开采业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他
运输设备制造业增加值同比分别增长 19.4%、11.5%、9.4%、9.2%，在主要
行业中增幅居前。金属制品业（8.6%）、食品制造业（8.6%）、医药制造业（5.9%）
增速均边际回升。非金属矿物制品业（-2.3%）、煤炭开采和洗选业（-4.4%）
同比降幅均小幅收窄。这反映出工业生产的改善并非全面扩散，传统周期性行
业仍受到需求不足、价格偏弱等因素制约，产业间景气分化较为明显。从产品
看，“人工智能+”带动产业链上下游产量较快增长，既体现在集成电路
（+21.9%）、电子元件（+15.2%）等核心硬件持续扩张，也体现在工业机器人
（+26.0%）、 3D 打印设备（+85.9%）等高端制造设备加快放量，以及地面通
信导航定向设备（+61.5%）等智能终端产品快速增长，反映出 AI产业发展正
由底层硬件向智能制造和多元化应用场景延伸。出口亦对工业生产形成正向带
动。10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，增速较上月加快 1.6

个百分点。装备制造业出口交货值增长 20.6%，增速较上月加快 3.4 个百分点，
其中，汽车、电气机械、电子行业出口交货值分别增长 52.2%、23.7%、15.3%，
对全部规上工业出口交货值增长的贡献率居各大类行业前三位，合计达
66.7%，说明出口对工业生产的拉动并未明显减弱，且支撑力量主要集中在装
备制造和电子产业链。综合来看，年内工业增加值增速或温和放缓，结构升级
趋势明确，高技术制造业和装备制造业是核心支撑。在强出口和稳投资政策发
力下，单月增速有望小幅回升，但难以扭转全年较前三季度小幅放缓的格局。
 整体投资降幅进一步扩大，重点基建领域维持较高景气。2026 年 1-10 月，全
国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分
点。根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月投资同比下降 17.84%，降幅较上月扩
大 8.16 个百分点。累计降幅扩大相对温和，但单月降幅明显加深，反映近期
投资下行压力仍在加大。基建投资方面，1-10 月广义基建投资同比下降 5.1%，

降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.9 个百分点；根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月广义基建
投资同比下降 17.63%，降幅较上月扩大 7.15 个百分点。资金端来看，10 月
地方政府新增专项债发行 3280 亿元，同比涨幅收窄至 14.13%，发行进度
88.07%，慢于去年同期 2.03 个百分点。专项债发行节奏放缓，叠加前期资金
向项目建设转化存在时滞，基建投资尚未形成回升趋势。尽管广义基建投资整
体负增长，但重点领域投资仍保持较快增长。1-10 月互联网和相关服务业投资
同比增长 48.4%，增速较 1-9 月份加快 1.7 个百分点，反映数字基础设施建设
仍有较强投资需求；航空运输业、水上运输业投资均增长 12.5%，电力供应业
投资增长 9.9%，说明“两重”项目建设及“六张网”相关领域投资仍在推进。
往后看，在四季度财政资金集中拨付、重大项目加速形成实物工作量的支撑下，
11、12 月单月投资增速环比有望小幅改善，带动全年降幅较 1-10 月略有收窄，
但全年转正的概率较低。基建投资中部分结构性高增领域可能受低基数、项目

集中开工影响，持续性需观察。同时，专项债剩余额度在年内可能对基建形成
一定支撑，但不宜高估其扭转深度负增的能力。
 地产供需两端低位运行，投资意愿与在建规模仍偏弱。2026 年 1-10 月，全国
房地产开发投资同比下降 21.6%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 1.0 个百分点；据历史数
据倒推测算，10月单月房地产开发投资同比下降 34.24%，降幅较上月扩大 7.04
个百分点，地产对整体投资的拖累有所加深，是投资端最大拖累项之一。供给
端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月房屋施工面积同比下降 32.5%，降幅较上
月扩大 13.9 个百分点，反映在建规模明显收缩；新开工面积同比下降 18.0%，
降幅收窄 8.1 个百分点，是供给端唯一边际改善项。新开工绝对增速仍深度负
增，且可能受低基数、项目集中开工影响，房企投资意愿仍未实质性修复；竣
工面积同比下降 39.1%，降幅扩大 2.4 个百分点，反映项目交付端压力或边际
上升。需求端，根据历史数据倒推测算，10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 20.7%，

降幅较上月收窄 1.9 个百分点；销售额同比下降 20.5%，降幅收窄 0.6 个百分
点。销售额降幅收窄幅度小于销售面积，意味着销售均价同比表现相对偏弱，
销售端的量价修复仍不充分。同时，销售端边际改善与投资、施工、竣工端恶
化形成背离。说明销售回款尚未有效转化为房企拿地、开工和施工强度，房企
流动性和投资意愿仍是核心约束。资金端，据历史数据倒推测算，10 月单月
房地产开发资金来源同比下降 22.0%，降幅较上月进一步扩大。其中国内贷款、
自筹资金、定金及预收款分别下降 33.0%、22.0%、15.6%，资金链仍在全面

3
收缩。往后看，年内稳地产政策以公积金改革、核心城市限购优化、购房贴息
等需求端工具为主，供给端主要依赖收储去库存，年底地产投资降幅仍在寻顶，
降幅收窄时点或推迟到 2027 年。
 整体企业资本开支意愿偏弱，高技术制造投资稳健增长。2026 年 1-10 月，制
造业投资同比下降 2.9%，降幅较 1-9 月扩大 0.8 个百分点；据历史数据倒推
测算，10 月单月制造业投资同比下降 9.32%，降幅较上月扩大 8.38 个百分点。
结合本月工业生产数据看，当前生产端的增长尚未充分转化为企业新增资本开
支，制造业投资意愿相对谨慎。分行业看，1-10 月高技术制造业投资同比增长
3.0%，其中电子电路制造业投资增长 59.6%，锂离子电池、集成电路制造业
投资分别增长 18.0%、13.8%；高技术服务业投资增长 5.9%，其中与人工智
能等新兴产业密切相关的信息服务业投资增长 20.5%，显示科技产业投资持续
向电子信息制造和人工智能相关服务领域集中。“两新”政策持续落地显效，

设备购置投资规模不断扩大。1-10 月，设备工器具购置投资同比增长 8.9%；
占全部投资的比重为 20.3%，比上年同期提高 3.2 个百分点。10 月单月，金属
制品业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业投资增速同比分别下降 8.5%和 2.5%，降
幅较上月分别收窄 8.2 和 6.8 个百分点。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设
备制造业、汽车制造业、通用设备制造业当月投资同比分别下降 0.2%、21.8%、
21.2%，降幅分别扩大 12.7、13.6、23.5 个百分点。装备制造业内部投资分化
加剧，终端需求偏弱、价格竞争加剧、产能利用率不足，以及前期抢出口和“以
旧换新”带来的需求前置，可能共同影响企业扩产意愿，促使企业资本开支趋
于谨慎。对比来看，10 月装备制造业工业增加值仍增长 10.6%，反映出当前
装备制造业呈现生产保持较强韧性、投资扩张意愿不足的特征，生产端与投资
端的背离进一步加深。往后看，高技术制造业投资动能有望延续，四季度制造
业投资单月同比有望转正，但全年增速明显转正难度较大；设备购置、电子电

路、锂电、集成电路和 AI 相关信息服务是主要支撑，汽车、通用设备和传统
制造或成为主要拖累。
 消费总量增长继续放缓，部分品类受到价格和低基数扰动。10 月单月，社会
消费品零售总额同比增长 0.4%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；扣除汽车后
的消费品零售额同比增长 2.5%，增速较上月收窄 1.4 个百分点。分项看，商
品零售同比增长 0.3%，增速较上月收窄 0.7 个百分点；餐饮收入同比增长
0.7%，增速收窄 1.4 个百分点，整体消费需求仍偏弱。分品类看，汽车类零售
额同比下降 21.4%，降幅较上月扩大 0.2 个百分点，成为社零增长的主要拖累，
反映居民大额耐用品消费意愿不足。通讯器材类零售同比增长 6.5%，增速较
上月大幅回落 24.6 个百分点，说明前期高增长后基数效应明显，补贴效应和
换机需求均有所减弱。金银珠宝类零售同比下降 28.7%，降幅较上月扩大 19.1
个百分点，主要因近期金价回调压低零售额读数，价格因素大于需求因素，也
反映出金银珠宝消费对金价波动高度敏感。石油及制品类零售同比由下降转为

上升 4.4%，主要受近期油价上涨叠加基数因素影响。家用电器和音像器材类
零售同比上升 2.5%，家具类零售同比下降 2.6%，增速均边际回升，或受去年
“以旧换新”退潮带来的低基数影响。尽管消费总量增速有所回落，消费结构
升级和服务消费韧性仍在延续。1-10 月，社会消费品和服务零售总额同比增长
2.5%，其中服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额 4.0 个百分点。往后看，
以旧换新政策边际效应减弱、居民消费意愿回升偏慢等因素或持续拖累社零整
体增速。年内消费韧性更多取决于服务消费能否延续较快增速，以及即时零售、
银发经济等新增长点能否对冲传统商品消费偏弱的拖累，托举社零中枢维持在
温和区间。
 风险提示：政策落地节奏不及预期风险，海外经济超预期波动风险。已公布的
宏观或产业政策在实际执行过程中，可能因配套措施不完善、部门协调不畅、
地方执行力度差异或市场环境变化等因素，导致其推进速度、覆盖范围或最终
效果慢于或弱于市场预期，从而影响政策目标的实现与经济修复的进程；主要
经济体的政治局势、经济数据或宏观政策出现意外变化，其幅度或频率超出市

场普遍预测。这种超预期波动可能引发全球金融市场动荡、贸易条件恶化或外
需急剧收缩，并通过金融、贸易及预期渠道向国内传导，增加我国外部环境的
复杂性与不稳定性。

分析师承诺 
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明 
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准，新三板市场以三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。 
公司评级 
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上 
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-10%与 10%之间 
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于-20%与-10%之间 
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-20%以下 
行业评级 
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数-5%以下

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

GCE’s 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 6%/1% below GSe/BBG 
consensus due mainly to (1) key AI customer’s product transition 
period (revenue in 3Q was 4/3% lower than GSe/BBG consensus), 
and (2) the increasing investment in R&D to improve its 
production capability on AI HDI and N+M MLB products), despite 
the much stronger than expected GM (0.8/1.8ppt higher than 
GSe/BBG consensus). For the net income in 3Q26, it was 5% below 
our bullish estimate, resulting from the weaker than expected 
operating profit, but it was 3% higher than BBG consensus, thanks to 
the lower than expected tax rate resulting from the increasing profit 
contribution from the low tax rate Thailand plant (tax rate was only 
at 25% in 3Q26 vs. BBG consensus at ~30%).  
For 4Q26, we expect the company’s revenue to grow by 7% QoQ, 
due to ongoing key AI customers’ product transition, and the fewer 
working days due to the holiday season. We expect GCE’s GM will 
further expand to 39.2% (up 0.5ppt QoQ or 5.5ppt YoY) thanks to 
the better product mix (high-end networking demand should ramp 
up more significantly from 4Q26, per GSe), despite the increasing 
cost from high-end CCL (see here).  
Investment view - GCE will continue to deliver a better margin 
profile with expanding market share in AI ASIC market 
We continue to expect GCE will hold the major share for Trainium 3 
(70%+ from 3Q26), and see an even better market share in the next 
generation Trainium 4 products (as key competitors are all unable to 
support high-end AI HDI products) after the company ramps up its 
AI HDI production yield rate. We also expect the company to start 
supplying the new TPU v8 PCB products, which should meaningfully 
improve the company’s market share in coming quarters (from <5% 
in 2026 to 20-30%+ in 2027), suggesting an even better GM outlook 
in the foreseeable future.  
On the other hand, the company will only expand its capacity

volume by ~20% in 2027, without considering the product mix improvement/ASP hike 
(we believe after considering the better ASP for next-gen AI products, the company’s 
capacity value should go up by 35%+ YoY in 2027), while we believe the AI PCB 
volume/area demand should at least increase by 50% YoY, implying a potential market 
share loss in coming quarters. We note Greater China peers are also expanding capacity 
in SEA regions to try to penetrate into GCE’s key AI ASIC markets, implying increasing 
competition in coming quarters, which could lead to less favorable ASP in the long term, 
despite no significant impact for now. However, considering GCE’s best in class AI PCB 
production technology and its proactive investment in the high-end PCB R&D for more 
than 10 years, as well as the company’s first mover advantage in the SEA PCB market, we 
expect GCE will continue to have opportunities to expand its business in the most 
high-end PCB market, suggesting a good GM outlook in the long term.  
Maintain Buy on GCE, with a new 12-m TP of NT$1,660 (from NT$1,800).

Earning and TP revision

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026/27/28E net income estimate by 9%/6%/5% to factor in the 
potential slower pricing hike outlook in the PCB industry, considering the increasing 
competition in the AI PCB industry (cut 2026/27/28E revenue estimate by 7/6/5%). 
Moreover, we believe GCE will need to invest more in R&D projects in the coming 
quarters to improve its HDI production capability, and expect the N+M PCB R&D 
investment will also drive OPEX in coming years, considering the increasing adoption 
rate from 1H27 (revise up 2027 OPEX to sales ratio by 0.3ppt).

### Page 4

TP revision 
Maintain Buy with a new 12-m TP of NT$1,660 (from NT$1,800), based on 15x P/E 
(down from 20x previously, still in line with AI PCB peers’ average valuation); we also 
rollover our valuation base from 2027E to 2H27-1H28E, and factor in our earnings 
revision.

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

GCE is a key cloud (server+switch) PCB supplier globally with 20%+ market share in 
2022. We expect cloud PCB market demand to grow at a 15% 2024-26E CAGR (far 
stronger than the overall RPCB+HDI market demand growth rate at 5% 2024-26E 
CAGR). We believe GCE’s proactive capacity expansion plan (33% in 2026) and high 
annualized ROI (300%+) will continue to drive the company’s revenue/margin level in the 
coming quarters/years. Additionally, we believe GCE can continue to see strong demand 
from the increasing content per server/switch, as we expect both markets to see a 
20-50%+ layer count increase after the generation upgrade (upgrade to 400G/Eagle 
Stream). We are constructive on GCE’s long-term growth opportunity given its solid 
position in the rapidly growing cloud PCB market, and we are Buy rated on the stock. 
GCE is trading higher than its historical P/E average level driven by strong AI server 
demand, but lower than the TW AI server players’ average. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,660 is based on a 15x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with AI PCB peers average valuation).  
Key downside risks: (1) weaker-than-expected AI server market demand; (2) delays in 
the company’s Nvidia OAM/UBB product qualification, (3) weaker-than-expected HDI 
demand, and (4) higher-than-expected ramp up cost for new capacity

Idea
See our 3Q26 results recap: 3Q26 Results Summary
4Q preview – revenue growth and margin expansion: We estimate 4Q revenue
will rise ~8% q/q, to NT$31.8bn (+94% y/y), driven by higher ASPs and shipments
deferred from Sep – Oct following flood-related logistics disruptions in Thailand.
We expect sequential revenue growth across all major product segments except PC/
NB, with networking delivering the largest increase, followed by AI/ASIC servers and

general servers. We forecast margin expansion of ~100bps q/q, to 39.7% (+600bps
y/y), supported by continued mix improvement and pricing adjustments to pass
through higher copper-clad laminate (CCL) costs. This should translate into net
profit of ~NT$7bn (+9% q/q, +130% y/y) and EPS of NT$13.82.
GCE’s Suzhou 2 and Thailand 2 plants remain on track to begin mass production
(MP) from 3Q27 and reach full ramp-up by end-CY27. Both facilities will support

advanced high-density interconnect (HDI) production for a key customer’s next-gen
compute board, which we think could adopt a 34-layer (3+28+3) advanced HDI
design using M9-grade CCL. While details remain subject to finalization, we expect
each plant to add monthly output value of ~NT$1bn or more. We also expect at
least one additional plant to begin advanced HDI MP in CY28, although details have
yet to be finalized. This would give GCE at least three plants capable of advanced

HDI production to meet demand from existing ASIC, and potential new, customers.
Reiterate OW with PT slightly lowered to NT$1,400, on our reduced medium-term
growth assumption (from 12% to 11%), as we bake in some conservatism on
concerns over a potential slowdown in data center build-out post the US midterm
election (see our Global Team's view), despite our marginally higher EPS estimates
for 2026-28e. Our new PT implies ~19x 2027e P/E, or ~14x 2028e P/E, slightly

lowered but still above GCE's 3Y P/E avg. of ~14x, which we view as fair, driven by its
capacity expansion plans and strong demand for AI servers and networking, with
room for margin expansion.

Idea
4
3Q26 Results Summary
• GCE reported 3Q26 gross margin of 38.7% (+400bp q/q, +310bp y/y), 180bp above
MSe and 190bps above the Street forecast. Management attributed the gross
margin improvement to successful cost pass-through, more favorable product mix
(AI mix was over 50% in the quarter; 800G switch mix also continued to increase
within Networking) and better profitability of the Thailand plant.
• Recall that we noted although GCE is able to fully pass through the additional

material costs to its customers, there will always be a timing difference (GCE
seeing higher input costs vs. reflecting the higher costs in new prices), which might
result in margin fluctuation.
• Operating expenses were again higher than expected, primarily due to higher
employee bonuses for talent retention and sales expenses. Thus, operating profit
came in at NT$8.8B (+36% q/q, +88% y/y), in line with MSe but 1% below
consensus forecasts, for operating margin of 29.6%.

• Non-operating losses of NT$238mn, primarily consisting of FX losses, were higher
than our and Street estimates of non-operating gains.
• Net profit was NT$6.4B (+33% q/q, +98% y/y), 2% above our estimate and    the
Street, for EPS of NT$12.39.
• The differences between net profit variances and EPS variances reflect share count
changes.

Price Target Discussion and Valuation Methodology
We lower our price target slightly, to NT$1,400.00 from NT$1,450.00: We derive our
price target, which is also our base-case scenario value, from a residual income (RI) model,
similar to the rest of our tech hardware coverage. The decrease in our price target is
driven by our slightly lowered medium-term growth assumption.
Our key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.0, equity risk premium of

8.7% and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate of 11% (lowered from 12%)
and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Our price target implies ~19x 2027e P/E, or ~14x 2028e P/E, vs. GCE's three-year historical
average 14.2x. We view the higher multiple as justified because GCE is showing longer-
term margin expansion, driven by a favorable mix shift toward more AI server PCBs and
high-speed networking PCBs (400G/800G/1.6T) alike. Some of its AI component peers are

trading at 20x+ 2027e multiple, which leads us to view GCE's valuation as fair.
Our bull- and bear-case scenario values decrease at  magnitudes similar to the decline
in our base-case value, to NT$1,995.00 and NT$850.00, respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 172: 261112_citi_msi

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_citi_msi.pdf

### Page 1

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins from ASP Pass-Through and Richer Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
MSI reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), 
beating Citi/cons by 27%/45% on stronger-than-expected margins and 
non-op gains. GPM of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8 ppts YoY) topped Citi/cons 
at 15.8%/14.5%, as ASP hikes passed through rising component costs while 
moving-avg inventory costs lagged, alongside a richer high-end product 
mix. For 4Q26E, we expect further ASP increases and a rising server 
contribution to largely offset seasonal shipment declines. Into 2027, we 
expect PC demand to stay weak and pass-through room to narrow as 
memory price increases moderately. We raise 2026E-28E earnings and our 
TP to NT$145 (10x 2027E EPS). We stay Neutral as the share price already 
reflects the pass-through upside.
3Q26: Margin-led beat on cost pass-through and better mix — MSI reported 
3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), beating Citi/cons forecasts 
by 27%/45%, mainly on stronger-than-expected margins and non-op gains. GPM 
of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8ppts YoY) came in above Citi/cons at 15.8%/14.5%. 
Mgmt. attributed the upside to 1) ASP increase, as MSI raised prices in line with 
suppliers’ cost increases while its moving-average inventory costs rose less; and 2) 
a higher mix of high-end products. Non-op gains, including FX gains of over 
NT$100mn, further boosted the bottom line.
Price hikes to continue; 2027 PC market to keep shrinking — We reiterate our view 
that 4Q26 shipments will decline seasonally from the 3Q26 peak. However, mgmt. 
expects rising raw material costs and continued pass-through to keep lifting ASPs, 
offsetting lower volumes and making the seasonal decline milder than in prior years. 
The server business should also contribute more, potentially reaching a high-single-
digit revenue share. Into 2027, we expect weak PC demand amid elevated costs, 
with MSI’s MB and PC shipments declining by over 10%. Pass-through room should 
narrow as memory price increases moderately, though margins should stay elevated 
for a while, as mgmt. expects memory supply to stay tight in the coming quarters.
Implications — We raise our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts by 6-27%, reflecting 
sustained strong component cost pass-through and higher margin assumptions. 
We adjust TP to NT$145 (from NT$140), implying 10x 2027E EPS (from 12x). We 
believe the share price already reflects MSI’s opportunity to pass component costs 
through to end products, and we expect limited upside going forward, given that the 
PC demand recovery remains highly uncertain. Maintain Neutral.
n
Michael Hung AC

### Page 6

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
13 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Micro-Star
Company description
Established in 1986 and listed in 1998, Taiwan-based MSI is a leading
OBM/ODM for gaming PCs, motherboards, graphic cards, industrial PCs, and
servers. MSI started business as a motherboard manufacturer but
transformed itself to become a leading gaming PC play in 2012 with a
comprehensive product portfolio across the gaming system and components.
MSI enjoys about 16% global share in the gaming NB industry, and is ranked

No.3 in graphic card shipments and No.3 in motherboard shipments globally.
Investment strategy
We rate MSI shares at Neutral as we anticipate earnings upward pressure
from the delince in PC and MB/VGA shipments in the near-term. While the
soaring component costs could be partially offset by price hike, we expect
muted demand and AI resource crowding to wiegh on margins upside. Further
upside maintains limited, though, until we see a meaningful recovery in core

gaming demand or a more substantial contribution from the server business.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$145 is based on 10x 2027E EPS, below the stock's five-
year average forward 12-month PE. This valuation reflects the earnings
downside risks from muted PC demand and margin pressure from soaring
component costs, while accounting for the time required for the server
business to achieve meaningful scale.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause MSI's shares to trade above our target price

include: 1) a sharper-than-expected recovery in high-end gaming demand;
and 2) faster-than-anticipated revenue contribution from the AI server
segment. Key downside risks include: 1) a deeper-than-expected contraction
in global PC shipments; 2) prolonged supply shortages for gaming GPUs due
to Ai resource crowding; and 3) inability to pass through surging memory and
CPU costs to price-sensitive consumers.
If you are visually impaired and would like to speak to a Citi representative regarding the details

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

M
Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Rachel L Zhang

Key Takeaways
Earlier this week, a key Japanese competitor issued a price increase notice to its
customers for electrodeposited copper foils.
This aligns with our  supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report.
We believe the  price hikes have positive implications for Co-Tech and expect Co-
Tech to follow with price increases as well.

However, since Co-Tech already implemented a ~10% price hike in October, we
don't expect it to announce a price hike of the same magnitude.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

研 究 報 告                
力旺(3529.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
先進與成熟製程同步發酵，營運逐季創高至 2027 年無虞 
 投資評價與建議 
公司地位居產業頂端，全球布局完善，先進製程貢獻推升與成熟製程漲價同步
推營運向上，此外，隨著Caliptra規範推升更多客戶導入公司產品，PUF有望
成為營運主⼒，⻑期營運成⻑無虞，近期股價因系統性風險影響反而浮現投資
價值。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26先進製程與成熟製程同步推升營收與獲利同創新高：受惠先進製程單價
提升更有助營收認列，及成熟製程漲價效應持續發酵，推估營收13.2億元，
QoQ+8.7%，EPS 9.45元。 
營運逐季成⻑將由2026年延續至2027年：展望未來，1)來自先進製程貢獻持
續發酵，由於先進製程單價更高，單⼀授權⾦單價數倍於平均，營收助益將更
大，未來權利⾦可望明顯提升；2)成熟製程回溫：因AI周邊的外溢效果帶動晶
圓代工廠產能利用率提升，自2Q26開始逐步漲價，而晶圓代工將持續漲價至
2027年，⼒旺自3Q26開始同步受惠；3)PUF自2Q25起，授權⾦及權利⾦貢獻
開始放大，其中大部分來自先進製程，隨著 AI 持續蓬勃發展，伴隨全球性資
安事件層出不窮，硬體安全需求有望加速進展，無論是Caliptra開始逐步建立
相關規範或Hardware Root of Trust正從選配變成標配，PUF潛在營運驚人。
整體而言，2026年營運逐季持續成⻑並續創新高將延續至2027年。 
 投資風險 
先進製程進展不如預期。

### Page 2

| 營運概況 
3Q26營收連四季創新高，獲利符合預期：1)受惠先進製程貢獻提升，授權⾦續創新高，其中PUF授權⾦1.6億元，
再創新高，顯示需求持續發酵；2)受惠成熟製程漲價，權利⾦7.7億元也創新高，其中Neofuse權利⾦5.4億元、PUF
權利⾦0.14億元、MTP權利⾦0.25億元，同創新高，整體3Q26營收12.1億元，QoQ+10.4%，明顯超越10億元規
模且連四季創新高，費用4.5億元則控制有成，EPS 8.56元，優於預期。 
4Q26先進製程與成熟製程同步貢獻，營收與獲利有望同創新高：10月營收7.26億元創新高，主要來自權利⾦5.94
億元(創新高)，除了先進製程單價提升更有助營收認列，及成熟製程漲價效應持續發酵(營收認列主要以晶圓代工
廠前⼀季營收為主，成熟製程主要自2Q26開始漲價)，推估營收13.2億元，QoQ+8.7%，EPS 9.45元。 
營運逐季成⻑將由2026年延續至2027年：展望未來，主要來自市場對先進製程技術、AI 相關應用、安全解決方
案的需求持續強勁，帶動授權⾦持續成⻑趨勢，及權利⾦成⻑動能可望加速，主要來自於新增先進製程應用帶來
的更高單價、新產品應用放量、PUF權利⾦貢獻擴大，以及具較高權利⾦的MTP產品占比提升，其中1)來自先進製
程貢獻持續發酵，由於先進製程單價更高，單⼀授權⾦單價數倍於平均，營收助益將更大，目前授權⾦NRE的佔
比，已經超過50%，未來權利⾦可望明顯提升，且3nm已有客戶量產成功，並持續朝2nm開發，未來對於權利⾦
貢獻將更顯著，因此3Q26無論是授權⾦或權利⾦同為新高或次高⽔準，未來將更具成⻑潛⼒；2)成熟製程回溫：
因AI周邊的外溢效果帶動晶圓代工廠產能利用率提升，自2Q26開始逐步漲價，而晶圓代工將持續漲價至2027年，
⼒旺自3Q26開始同步受惠，此外，既有應用升級帶動權利⾦單價提升(如16nm 折疊式手機OLED DDI，PMIC從
8吋轉到55nm)，對於營收結構仍以成熟製程為主的⼒旺將是⼀大福⾳；3)PUF自2Q25起，授權⾦及權利⾦貢獻
開始放大，其中大部分來自先進製程，隨著 AI 持續蓬勃發展，目前Security 並非主要考量，而Caliptra僅開始逐
步建立相關規範，但近期全球性資安事件層出不窮，硬體安全需求有望加速進展，此外，Hardware Root of Trust 
正從選配變成標配，PUF潛在營運驚人，隨著製程進入2nm，晶片設計與製造成本持續提高，客戶對面積、功耗、
可靠度以及符合安全標準的要求也更為嚴格，NeoFuse及PUF-based security IP的競爭優勢也更加明顯。整體而
言，2026年營運逐季持續成⻑並續創新高將延續至2027年。 
營運逐季創高至2027年，中⻑期成⻑無虞：公司地位居產業頂端，全球布局完善，先進製程貢獻推升與成熟製程
漲價同步推營運向上，此外，隨著Caliptra規範推升更多客戶導入公司產品，PUF有望成為營運主⼒，⻑期營運成
⻑無虞，近期股價因系統性風險浮現投資價值，投資建議維持買進，目標價3,550元。

高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利
率創歷史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700
萬美金，除了擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合
作關係。展望 2027 年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高
毛利的 Power 產品最為明顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表
現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026 年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年
EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷史 PE 區間為 10-35x，
考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔憂資料中心建置速
度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部屬之風險，
因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。 
⚫ 公司基本資料 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
貿聯-KY(3665)成立於 1996 年，為全球連接線大廠，總部位於美
國矽谷，生產據點則廣泛分布在全球 37 地。產品應用包含數據通
訊、工廠自動化、半導體設備、醫療、車用等，2025 年工業佔 35%、
通訊 42%、車用 11%、電器 11%、其他 1%。銷售主要為美國 20%、
中國 20%、德國 16%、馬來西亞 7%、台灣 3%、其他 34%。

Interplex 正式併表，產品組合有望再度優化 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.5 億元，M+26.9%，Y+80.0%，自 9 月起完成
對 Interplex 的收購，10 月已完整貢獻營收，單月貢獻金額高達
6700 萬美金，約 20 億台幣，由於 Interplex 既有客戶與貿聯本身
有所重疊，然而產品項目卻有所不同，預計在透過集團整合能力下
將進一步推動公司在客戶群內滲透率的提升。此外貿聯本身新規格
的 Power 產品也持續提高出貨，研究部預期產品組合有望再度優
化，進一步推升獲利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 26Q4 營
收 349.1 億元，Q+25.3%，Y+76.5%，毛利率 33.84%，稅後淨利
56.7 億元，Q+32.0%，Y+106.8%，EPS 28.39 元；2026 年營收
1069.3 億元，Y+50.1%，毛利率 31.83%，稅後淨利 152.1 億元，
Y+69.0%，EPS 76.23 元。

2027 年 HPC 產品維持價量齊揚，光通訊產品 2028 年有望發酵 
隨著 Vera Rubin 進入放量，Rack Busbar 升級至 5000A，Power 
Whip 規格從 60A 升級至 100A 且用量增加，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
開始貢獻營收，另外 Interplex 的加入所帶來的產品優化和客戶關
係的綁定，研究部認為貿聯 HPC 事業仍將迎來顯著的價量齊揚增
長。光傳輸領域上貿聯也持續耕耘，除了新富生 MPO 相關產品，
OCP 上與 ams Osram 的合作也意味著公司已著手布局下一代
Scale up 的通訊方案，研究部看好 wide-and-slow 架構在未來的導
入機會以及市場，預計 2028 年後的發酵有望帶來營運成長動能。
綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2027 年營收 1608.0 億元，Y+50.4%，

結論 
高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利率創歷
史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700 萬美金，除了
擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合作關係。展望 2027
年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高毛利的 Power 產品最為明
顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將
會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026
年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年 EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷
史 PE 區間為 10-35x，考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔
憂資料中心建置速度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部
屬之風險，因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

## Document 274: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

### Page 6

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

### Page 8

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

 .

 increase its attractiveness

 and is supporting economic

 and large FX reserves
 . 
 A major

 the

 the

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH DESPITE 
STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS 
◼ 
 In October 2026, the IMF revised upward 
 India’s  
medium
 -
 term growth 
 forecast 
 from 6.5% to 6.8%  
per year
 . Nonetheless, 
to achieve such strong growth performance, 
 India will have to adapt to the challenges posed by artificial intelligence  
(AI)
, 
while its infrastructure and education system are not prepared for it.  
◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i
 ntegration into 
global value chains.  
◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i
 ) adapting the services sector to AI and ii) accelerating industrial development. 
Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo
u
r market 
 rigidities  
and the mismatch between training and business needs are holding 
back both the creation of quality jobs and industrial development.  
◼ 
 Low net foreign direct investment is a 
 major  
 obstacle. While 
 India attracts 
 new 
 FDI, retention is weak
 : 
 despite 
improvements in logistics, governance issues and regulatory barriers have 
 led 
 disinvestments to triple over the past five 
years.  
◼ 
 India 
 may fail in completing its 
 transition to a higher
 -
 value, more industrialised and inclusive economy if reforms do not 
accelerate 
 significantly
 . Structural change
 s 
could lag behind pressures from its rapidly growing population and 
 fast 
global 
technological advances
 . 
02 November 2026

### Page 3

EcoInsight

economic
 -
 research.bnpparibas.com

3

INDIA
’S COMPLEX POSITION 
IN INTERNATIONAL TRADE 
Low 
attractiveness  
due to unfavourable business environment and limited integration in global value chains  
◼ 
 Despite very strong growth, India's attractiveness is low relative to the size of its economy. The stock of foreign direct 
investment stood at only 15% of GDP in Q2 2026 compared to 52% of GDP in Vietnam and around 67% of GDP in Thailand 
and Malaysia.  
◼ 
 This lack of appeal can be attributed to 
 India’s still unfavo
 u
rable 
 business environment and 
 its 
 limited integration into 
global value chains. India’s global market share remain
 s 
 low (1.9% of total 
 world 
 exports  
 of goods
 )
, 
 especially in 
comparison with that of China (16.1% of total exports).  
◼ 
 India has not benefited from the AI boom
 , 
since its exports of AI
 -
 related goods remain modest (0.5% of its total exports)
 . 
However, 
 it has benefited from the “China +1” strategy adopted by 
 international 
 companies seeking to diversify their 
production sources. Smartphone exports by India have increased 6.3
 -
 fold over the past five years and accounted for almost 
6% of global exports in 2025 compared to less than 1% five year
 s 
earlier.  
To 
increase its a

pharmaceuticals and chemical
 s

 India bilateral trade agreement has not yet been concluded. The two sides reached a framework for an interim 
Agreement in February 2026, under which the US would apply an 18% reciprocal tariff on Indian goods, with further 
reductions subject to the

 are 
stalled. Key sticking 
points include market access, particularly 
 in 
India’s sensitive agricultural sector.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 . 
 In 2025, external accounts remained solid 
despite the significant increase in US tariffs. In 
 H1 
 2026, they held up well against rising energy prices. Although the 
current account deficit is expected to widen for the current fiscal year as a whole, it should remain contained, and foreign 
exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally 
 low level of foreign direct investment (although it is rising) and the increased reliance on 
portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.  
◼

 .

rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)
 . The trade 
deficit widened significantly but principally because of the strong increase in machinery and equipment imports rather

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.  
◼ 
 Banks’ 
 liquidity 
 is 
 comfortable
 . 
It deteriorated slightly in H1 2026 because credit growth outpaced deposit growth
 ; t
he 
liquidity coverage ratio decli
 ned to 
a still solid 
 124% 
and 
the loan
 -
 to
-
 deposit ratio increased to 83% in Q2 2026 from 79.1% 
a year earlier. RBI interventions to support the rupee 
 also 
 drained liquidity
 . Moreover
 , the deposit mix shifted from low
 -
cost current and savings accounts (CASA) to higher
 -
 cost term deposits, pressuring net interest margins. However, since 
June 2026, liquidity has improved significantly due to RBI measures to attract foreign
 -
 currency non
 -
 re
sident (FCNR) 
deposits.  
◼ 
 Profitability remains satisfactory. 
 It 
 has declined since the start of the year, primarily due to narrowing net interest 
margins, but remains satisfactory. In Q2 2026, 
 the 
 return on assets (ROA) and return on equity (ROE) stood at 1.6% and 
13.0%, respectively (IMF data).  
◼ 
 P
rivate
 -
 sector  
 debt 
 is moderate
 , 
 but its increase 
 needs to be monitored.  
 It stood 
 at 105.1% of GDP in Q1 2026 (BIS  
data
), compared to 100.1% five years earlier
 , which is 
 lower than in other emerging Asian economies.  
Non
-

 e 
debt was 56% of GDP in Q1 2026. Listed firms’ financial positions continued to strengthen amid 
surging profits, improving their ability to service debt. In Q2 2026, the 
 average

from 6.1 a year earlier. However, the share of companies with an ICR below 1 (insufficient earnings to cover interest costs) 
increased to 18.5%. Stress remain
 s 
 concentrated in micro
 -

 e.g.

most exposed to oil

Household leverage requires monitoring. Although still moderate, household debt increased to 49.1% of GDP in Q1 2026, 
up from 42.7% five years earlier, driven largely by non
 -
 housing retail loans (58.4% of total borrowing).  
◼ 
 Monetary 
 policy 
 tightening. 
 Due to rising  
inflation (+5.5% year
 -
 on
-
 year in September) and downward pressure on the 
rupee, the RBI raised its policy rate by 25 basis points to 5.5% in October 2026 under a "calibrated tightening" stance.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector should remain resilient

 Due to rising energy prices, a poor

 -

and small enterprises.

arly warning signs are emerging

(

 )
. 
This could lead to new

## Document 358: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio 
Foundation tactical asset allocation

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We did not materially alter our real PCE forecast, and expect consumer spending growth to
continue.
Consumer spending has held up remarkably well this year despite higher gasoline costs weighing on
household purchasing power. While we do not expect the recent above-trend near-4% annualized
pace of real consumption growth to be sustained, we see consumers continuing to spend at a healthy

clip into next year. A firmer income backdrop revealed by recent revisions, continued support from
household wealth and an expected recovery in purchasing power as inflation slows should keep
consumption on solid footing. We expect real consumption growth to remain north of 2% in the final
quarter of the year and roughly match this year’s average pace in 2027.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products, and Inventories
• A stronger-than-expected capex outturn in Q3 has revealed a slightly firmer trend headed into

next year.
Business investment continues to be driven by the high-tech buildout, with real equipment spending
stronger than expected in Q3 and the latest data suggest a firmer trend. We have not materially
changed our outlook following the Democratic sweep in the midterms. Our forecast already assumes
some moderation in high-tech investment next year and a smaller contribution to GDP growth as the
buildout matures. Beyond high-tech, we expect only a modest broadening in capex spending over the

coming year.
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Investment: Residential
• We have not made major changes to our outlook for residential investment. High mortgage rates
and challenging affordability conditions look set to continue pressuring home sales and new single-
family construction.
Not much has changed in the way of residential investment. Mortgage rates have come in somewhat,

but are still elevated above 7.0%. Building permits are stuck in a low gear, with single-family projects
continuing to moderate against a backdrop of weak buyer demand and elevated inventories.
Multifamily permits have picked up modestly, reflecting renewed strength in apartment demand.
Modestly lower mortgage rates and stabilizing apartment market conditions should help put a floor
beneath residential investment and bring about modest growth over the course of next year.

Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• Structures investment is likely to maintain healthy momentum amid robust data center, power, and
related nonresidential construction.
4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Structures investment accelerated notably in Q3. The bulk of this momentum can be traced to data
centers and power, however, activity also appears to be broadening out. As examples, private spending
on communication and transportation infrastructure have perked up and manufacturing construction
appears to be stabilizing on increased semiconductor investment. The downturn in commercial
construction has also softened as data centers shore up demand for warehousing. Although new

regulations may soften the pace of data center starts, there are more than enough projects already in
the pipeline to support healthy structures investment through next year.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports should remain a fairly persistent drag on U.S. real GDP growth amid continued
imports.
A surge in imports, particularly of high-tech goods like computers, caused net exports to subtract

more than 3 percentage points from Q3 GDP growth. We expect trade to remain a drag in Q4, though
with little data in hand, that largely reflects where trade flows ended last quarter and an assumption
of another sizable gain in imports. Looking ahead, continued strength in high-tech investment should
keep import growth elevated and the trade deficit wide, leaving net exports a persistent drag on U.S.
growth. Exports remain more volatile, but imports are increasingly the bigger driver of the trade story.

Labor Market
• Labor demand has improved modestly in recent months. We now expect payroll growth to average
65K per month in 2027, and the unemployment rate to hold steady at 4.1% over the next four
quarters.
The October jobs report reinforced our view that the labor market remains solid. The pickup in job
growth this year aligns with purchasing manager surveys that point to expanding employment across
the goods and services sectors. At the same time, the historically low trends in initial jobless claims and

the layoff rate suggest employers broadly remain reluctant to shed workers. Structurally, firms appear
focused on retaining existing staff and investing heavily in AI adoption while remaining selective about
adding to head count—a dynamic that should keep the low-hire, low-fire environment largely intact.
We expect payroll growth to average around 65K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate
of breakeven job growth. That should keep the unemployment rate steady at 4.1% through next year

and labor market conditions generally consistent with full employment.
Inflation
• We look for the core PCE deflator to rise 3.3% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to
2.6% in 2027.
The October inflation report reinforced the persistence of price pressures, with upside surprises
emerging across a range of categories. Although gasoline and diesel prices have receded in recent
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
weeks, earlier increases in transportation costs are still working their way through supply chains. Food
inflation picked up in October and looks poised to strengthen further. The outlook for energy prices
remains highly uncertain and represents the largest swing factor for inflation, in our view. Within the
core, we continue to expect goods inflation to moderate in 2027 as tariff-related pressures fade
further and demand tied to the AI buildout cools. Services inflation, however, is likely to prove stickier.

Health care services prices have been running hot and should continue to face upward pressure from
the aging population, and primary shelter inflation looks to be stabilizing. Taken together, we expect
inflation to remain above the Fed's 2% target through next year and look for the core PCE deflator to
end 2027 at 2.6% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis.

Fiscal Policy
• The lame duck session of Congress probably will not yield a budget reconciliation bill or a debt

ceiling resolution. The December 11 government funding deadline probably will be punted to Q1/
early Q2 via another continuing resolution.
• We project a federal budget deficit of $2.0 trillion for FY 2027, similar to FY 2026 in dollar terms
but a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
Assuming that Congress does not increase or suspend the debt ceiling in the next six weeks, the
incoming Democratic Congress will need to negotiate a resolution with President Trump in 2027.

Right now, it looks like Treasury will hit the debt ceiling around early March. At that point, Treasury can
lean on extraordinary measures and its cash on hand to last for about 7-8 months, putting the debt
ceiling "X date" somewhere around early Q4. We suspect the debt ceiling/annual budget negotiations
will include a lift to defense and nondefense discretionary government spending for FY 2027/2028,
but we doubt the bump will be enough to meaningfully move our economic growth or deficit forecasts.

For now, our working assumption is that divided government will not produce any major new fiscal
expansions/contractions over the next two years, leaving the budget deficit picture as status quo until
after the 2028 election.
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our baseline forecast is for another 25 bp rate hike to the fed funds rate in December, followed by
an extended hold from the FOMC through 2027. The risks are tilted toward 1-2 more hikes early

next year.
• Our forecast for the 10-year Treasury yield at year-end 2027 is 4.85%.
The recent inflation data seem to make a hike in December a lock so long as financial conditions don't
completely implode in the coming weeks. The core CPI came in about 8 bps above consensus at 0.3%
for October, and a 0.4% on core PCE is plausible, depending on how the PPI data shake out. This should
put the FOMC's hawks back in the driver's seat after the October skip.

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our expectation is that
core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see clear signs of demand-side inflation in
the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation data reset the
clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates some modest upside

risk to our base case for the fed funds rate next year.
